{"parentUuid":"c9023bf6-a822-47d9-a197-8ca63c7cd14d","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a88ff7b2e1ac62eee","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- artifact-planner: Produce a self-contained, navigable HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPLIT STORYBOARD: each scene pairs a rich UI/mockup (left) with a before→after Mermaid diagram of the structural change (right), plus a \"what this lets you do\" line and an \"under the hood\" strip. Embeds a machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, walk through, or visualize any multi-step flow: onboarding, checkout, auth, OAuth integrations, wizards, subscription cancellation, push notifications, support ticket lifecycles, AI chat execution, CI/CD pipelines, refund flows, admin features, migrations, webhook lifecycles, or anything else with sequenced stages and decision points. Trigger on \"create an artifact for…\", \"plan this flow\", \"make a flow plan\", \"visualize this flow\", \"show me the stages of…\", \"mock up the flow for…\", \"I want to plan X visually\", \"build a walkthrough of…\", or any time the user has just described a feature/flow and would benefit from a single-page visual + structured spec they can revisit, share, or drop back into a new chat. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- artifact-planner-canvas: Produce a self-contained HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPATIAL CANVAS: the WHOLE plan on one pannable/zoomable Figma-style board — frontend mockups in a top band, each stage's before→after Mermaid diagram directly beneath (dashed spine connectors), branches as tinted lanes with fork/rejoin curves, decisions and open questions as sticky notes placed next to the stages they affect, and double-click-anywhere comment stickies. This is the CANVAS variant of `artifact-planner` (which pages one scene at a time as a split storyboard): prefer the canvas when the user wants to see everything at once, compare stages spatially, or leave free-floating comments; prefer the paged storyboard for linear walkthroughs and presentations. Embeds the same machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, or visualize any multi-step flow (onboarding, checkout, auth, wizards, CI/CD pipelines, migrations, webhook lifecycles…) AND signals a canvas: \"canvas artifact\", \"spatial plan\", \"put the plan on a canvas/board\", \"Figma-style board\", \"show me the whole flow at once\", \"map it out spatially\" — or when they ask for the canvas version of an existing artifact-plan. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- auto-rough-cut: Turn a folder of raw class-video recordings — separately recorded pristine voice tracks (Audacity .wav) plus their screen/camera videos (.mp4) — into a draggable Adobe Premiere Pro FCP7 XML that is silence-cut to Ray's Recut preferences, has the pristine audio synced and linked to the video, transcribes every kept chunk, and flags repeated takes / filler / breaths in PURPLE for a fast review pass. Use this skill whenever Ray points at a folder of recording pairs and wants a rough cut, first assembly, or \"silence cut\", or says things like \"cut the silences\", \"sync my audio and video and remove the dead air\", \"make a Premiere XML from these recordings\", \"apply my Recut process / record preferences\", \"flag the retakes\", \"auto rough-cut this video\", \"turn my Audacity audio + screen recording into a timeline\", or stages new recordings under a \"[Class Videos]\" / to-film folder. Trigger it even if Recut, transcription, or retakes aren't all named — any \"remove the silence and give me a Premiere XML I can still drag around\" request for paired audio+video is this skill. Distinct from recut-xml-fixer (which only repairs an XML Recut already exported); this skill does the whole cut from raw media.\n- auto-spec: Turn a feature brief (inline text or a stub plan file) into an implementation-ready plan via an autonomous two-agent interview, an external-API research verification pass, and a two-agent codebase-grounded planning pass — and, in its bootstrap mode, build/refresh the personalized digital twin that powers that interview by mining your Claude Code conversation history. Use when the user says \"auto spec\", \"spec autopilot\", \"auto interview\", \"flesh out this plan\", \"/auto-spec <file or brief>\", or otherwise wants a plan written without manually answering questions; ALSO use for \"create my auto-spec\", \"clone my brain\", \"make a spec twin\", \"personalize auto-spec\", \"build my decision profile\", or any request to analyze conversation history for decision patterns. Produces a single plan file with Feature Brief, Spec Decisions, External Research, Codebase Map, and phased Implementation Plan — ready to hand to `/implement-task` — or, in bootstrap mode, a refreshed `decision_profile.md` + `qa_examples.json` digital twin.\n- chronicle\n- codex-consult: OpenAI Codex CLI wrapper — three modes. Code review: independent diff review via\ncodex review with pass/fail gate. Challenge: adversarial mode that tries to break\nyour code. Consult: ask codex anything with session continuity for follow-ups.\nThe \"200 IQ autistic developer\" second opinion. Use when asked to \"codex review\",\n\"codex challenge\", \"ask codex\", \"second opinion\", or \"consult codex\".\n- improve-claude-md: improve a CLAUDE.md file using <important if> blocks to improve instruction adherence\n- interview: Relentlessly interrogate a plan, decision, or idea one question at a time until the user and Claude reach a genuinely shared understanding. Use this whenever the user wants to stress-test their thinking, pressure-test a plan, be challenged on an idea, or says any of \"interview me\", \"grill me\", \"grill this\", \"poke holes in this\", \"stress-test this\", \"ask me everything\", \"interrogate me\", \"what am I missing\", or \"/interview\". Also reach for it when the user drops a half-formed plan and asks for questions before you build anything, or when a request is underspecified enough that guessing would waste real work. Do not write code, files, or plans while this skill is active until the user says the picture is complete.\n- slackbot-message: Send Slack messages to a named channel via a bot user. Use this skill whenever you need to notify Ray on Slack, post a status update, alert a channel, deliver a short summary, or when he asks you to \"post to slack\", \"message #channel\", \"send a slack message\", \"ping slack\", \"slack me\", \"let me know on slack\", or names a specific Slack channel by name. Also use it after long-running tasks finish if Ray asked for Slack notifications. The bot is invited to the relevant channels, so the script can post to any channel by its human-readable name (no need to know channel IDs).\n- spec-developer-v2: Interview the user about a rough idea or brief with sharp, non-obvious product and technical questions, then write a concise implementation-ready spec. Use when the user wants to spec something out, develop a vague idea into requirements, or turn a product concept into a detailed plan.\n- sync-skills: Commit and push GLOBAL skill changes to the personal skills git repo\n(github.com/ray-amjad/private-skills). Auto-detects what changed in\n~/.claude/skills/, stages the changes, generates a meaningful commit message,\nand pushes to origin main. Use this skill whenever you finish creating or\nediting a global skill, or when the user says \"sync skills\", \"push skills\",\n\"save skills\", \"/sync-skills\", or asks to commit skill changes to git.\nIMPORTANT: Only trigger this skill when working at the project level (i.e., when\nthe working directory is a project repo, not from within ~/.claude/skills/ itself).\nDo NOT use this for project-scoped skills (e.g. skills under a project's own\n.claude/skills/, like ray-os's sentence-mining) — those are committed to that\nproject's repo, not here.\n- ultragoal\n- vercel-logs: Check Vercel production logs, errors, and build output. Use this skill whenever you need to\ndiagnose a deployment error, check for runtime errors, investigate 500s, or the user asks\n\"check the logs\", \"what's the error\", \"why is it broken\", \"check vercel logs\", or any\nrequest to look at Vercel deployment/runtime/build logs. Also trigger on `/vercel-logs`.\n\nCRITICAL: Also invoke this skill BEFORE running any `vercel logs` command yourself —\neven when no one asked you to. The CLI has non-obvious flag interactions (implicit\nfollow mode, stderr-only output, --since rejection) that cause hangs or silent failures\nif you guess at flags. If you're about to type `vercel logs` in a Bash call, load this\nskill first.\n- vercel-monitor: Watch a Vercel deployment in the background and notify when it reaches Ready/Error/Canceled.\nUse this skill whenever the user pushes to main, runs `git push`, deploys to Vercel, or asks\nto \"monitor the deploy\", \"watch the build\", \"check if it deployed\", or anything similar —\neven if they don't explicitly say \"Vercel\". Also trigger on `/vercel-monitor`. This replaces\nolder patterns of spawning a haiku subagent or polling via the Vercel MCP, both of which\nwaste tokens and context on large projects.\n- anki-cleanup\n- binary-explorer: Reverse-engineer any desktop application OR Claude Code's own binary to understand how it works behind the scenes — the APIs it calls, the internal flow, the models/providers it uses, its feature flags, config constants, embedded prompts, IPC protocols. For general apps it works on Electron apps (Wispr Flow, Slack, Discord, VS Code, Cursor, Notion, Linear…), native macOS .app bundles, single-executable bundles (Bun/Node/Deno/Go SEA), and raw Mach-O / ELF / PE binaries. It ALSO reverse-engineers Claude Code's own binary — it knows Claude Code's mangled variable conventions, feature-flag patterns, and embedded-prompt layout. Use this skill whenever the user points at a desktop app and wants to know \"how does X work under the hood\", \"what API does it call\", \"how does it do Y\", \"what's the flow for Z\", \"reverse engineer this app\", \"figure out how this feature works\", \"what endpoints does it hit\", \"does it run locally or in the cloud\", \"what model does it use\", or names a specific app path (.app, .exe, a binary). ALSO use it whenever the user asks about a Claude Code feature, feature flag, hidden toggle, internal prompt, or wants to understand how something in Claude Code works under the hood — especially unreleased, gated, or undocumented functionality — or says \"how does X work in the binary\", \"what does this feature flag do\", \"find the prompt for X\", \"reverse engineer X\", or mentions a specific codename/flag. Also trigger when the user wants to audit an app's network behavior, understand its architecture, or ext…\n- class-scriptwriter: Turn a raw idea, source tweet, video transcript, feature announcement, or rough outline into a polished video script for Ray's Agentic Coding School classes. Use this skill whenever the user says \"write a class script\", \"turn this into a class video\", \"script this for the [business/claude-code/prompt-engineering/skills/context-engineering/techniques/claude-chat/claude-cowork/correction] class\", \"draft an agentic coding school video\", or whenever the user is staging content in `/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os/projects/agentic-coding-school/to-film/` and wants it written up. Default to this skill for any new file destined for the `to-film/` tree, even if the user does not name it explicitly. Produces an essay-style markdown script with the correct frontmatter, horizontal rules between sections, inline image embed placeholders, and a Demo section, written in Ray's direct second-person voice.\n- content-cascade\n- discord-archive\n- excalidraw-bundle: Bundle a set of section images into a single .excalidraw file laid out as a grid — one column per section in document order, the section's variations stacked vertically — and save it to ~/Downloads, ready to drag into excalidraw.com or the Obsidian Excalidraw plugin.\n- excalidraw-codex: Generate excalidraw-style explanation images using the Codex CLI (codex exec --json) instead of a paid API. Use when the user wants visual explanations, diagrams, or illustrations in the hand-drawn excalidraw aesthetic and asks for the codex path. Codex's bundled image_gen tool runs through the logged-in account, so no GEMINI_API_KEY is required and it's often faster than the Gemini route. This is the RECOMMENDED DEFAULT path for excalidraw image generation: it needs no API key and runs through the logged-in Codex account. Default to codex whenever the user does not explicitly ask for the Gemini route.\n- excalidraw-codex-simple\n- excalidraw-deck: Build hand-drawn, Excalidraw-style HTML slide decks that explain a concept through sketch-aesthetic vector diagrams — rough.js strokes on a dark canvas, full-bleed, one diagram per slide with a caption underneath, arrow-key and tap navigation. Use this skill whenever the user wants explainer slides, a concept deck, diagram slides, or a talk/video presentation in the hand-drawn sketch look, or says things like \"make slides for X\", \"build a deck for X\", \"add a slide in this style\", \"turn this into a sketch/excalidraw-style deck\", \"another slide like the workflow deck\", or points at an existing rough.js deck and wants more in the same style. This is for self-contained vector-diagram decks — NOT the Claude Code terminal-aesthetic slides (use cc-terminal-slides) and NOT raster image generation (use excalidraw-gen, which calls an image model). Reach for this skill proactively for any \"explain this concept on slides\" request, even when the user does not say the word \"excalidraw\".\n- extract-wisdom: Extract structured wisdom, insights, and actionable takeaways from any content — podcasts, articles, videos, transcripts, books, or conversations. Use this skill whenever the user asks to \"extract wisdom\", \"summarize insights\", \"pull out key ideas\", \"what are the takeaways\", \"extract the best parts\", \"distill this content\", \"what did I learn from this\", or provides a transcript/article and asks for a structured breakdown. Also trigger when the user pastes or references long-form content and wants the signal extracted from the noise — even if they don't use the word \"wisdom\" specifically. Inspired by Daniel Miessler's Fabric pattern. Has a BATCH MODE for running this extraction across a whole YouTube channel in bulk: lists videos with yt-dlp, filters by duration, downloads transcripts, then fans out one extract-wisdom subagent per video in batches of 5, writing one wisdom file per video to disk. Trigger batch mode whenever the user wants wisdom/insights/takeaways from a YouTube channel in bulk: \"extract wisdom from this channel\", \"go through @<handle>'s long videos\", \"summarise everything on <channel>\", \"give me the best ideas from <creator>'s podcast\", \"process a whole channel into wisdom notes\", or \"I want to read the highlights from every video on @<handle> longer than 20 minutes\".\n- humanizer\n- idea-foundry\n- image-cleanup: Clean up unreferenced images in Obsidian vault folders. Deletes unused images, renames excalidraw files with descriptive names based on image content (using vision), and moves everything to vault root images/ folder. Use after picking favorites from excalidraw-gen batches. Triggers on \"clean up images\", \"delete unused images\", \"tidy up images\", \"image cleanup\", or \"/image-cleanup\".\n- linkedin: All LinkedIn tasks — writing posts (text or carousel), scouting sources to post about, checking post performance, browsing the feed, or analyzing competitors. Also triggers on \"check post engagement\", \"update post metrics\", or reviewing social media performance. Triggers on requests like \"write a newsletter\", \"draft a newsletter about...\", \"help me write my weekly email\", \"newsletter style\", or when the user provides a topic and wants it turned into a newsletter format.\n- rakuten-hotels\n- recut-xml-fixer: Reshape an FCP7 XML exported from a silence-cutting app (Recut, AutoEdit, Vika, etc.) so Adobe Premiere Pro imports it cleanly with the user's pristine external audio (e.g. Audio.wav from Audacity) linked to the video instead of the camera/screen-recording's own audio. Use this skill whenever the user points at an .xml or sequence file from Recut, AutoEdit, or any auto-cutter and wants to swap in their own audio track, drop the redundant on-camera audio, fix vertical alignment between video and audio cuts, or fix a Premiere \"import failed\" error on a Recut-style XML. Triggers on phrases like \"fix this Recut XML\", \"swap the audio in this premiere XML\", \"align the cuts to my Audio.wav\", \"remove the loom audio track\", \"merge V1 and A1 in this XML so they're linked\", or any reference to a workflow combining a silence-cutter app's XML with a separately recorded pristine audio file.\n- sentence-mining: Build and maintain Japanese sentence-mining cards for Anki, fully self-contained (no AnkiMorphs install required) and configurable per user via a one-time `/sentence-mining setup`. Six modes, and the skill ASKS which one you want on every launch. (1) Video mode — paste any Instagram reel, YouTube video/Short, TikTok, Twitter video, or local file → yt-dlp + AssemblyAI + SudachiPy + a built-in i+1 known-word diff produces draft cards. (2) Bank mode — give a list of target words → find the best example sentence for each through one canonical cascade (Immersion Kit → Nadeshiko → sentencesearch → kotu → your locally-indexed subs2srs .apkg banks), re-ranked by your own i+1, with native audio and a screenshot where the source ships one. (3) Replace mode — fix existing cards whose example sentence is bad (too short, a fragment, incomprehensible): pull a better sentence through the same cascade, edit the card in place (archiving the old sentence to a previous_versions field), and rehabilitate the card so you re-learn it fresh. (4) Leech mode — work the cards Anki flagged as leeches, but filtered by whether they are STILL failing: `tag:leech` is a scar Anki never removes, so most of a leech list is usually cards that recovered months ago (43 of Ray's 78 sat at 21-338 day intervals). Cards below the interval threshold get a fresh sentence through the cascade plus full rehabilitation (reps/lapses zeroed, due today); recovered cards just have the tag stripped with their scheduling untouched; words rarer than a JPDB frequen…\n- transcribe-youtube-video: Transcribe audio or video into a clean .txt + timestamped .srt + YouTube chapter timestamps. Re-encodes any WAV/MP4 to a small MP3 before upload, mines on-screen text with OCR to seed AssemblyAI key terms, runs AssemblyAI Universal-3 Pro speech-to-text, then generates punchy chapter markers from the SRT. Use whenever the user wants to transcribe a video or audio file, generate subtitles/captions or an SRT, create a YouTube transcript or chapters, or convert a recording to text. Also trigger when the user has a WAV/MP4/MOV and wants text out of it, even without saying 'transcribe'.\n- ungate-claude-code-feature: Enable a gated, experimental, or staged-rollout Claude Code feature on your own machine by finding its GrowthBook feature flag in the binary and force-enabling it through a Proxyman interception rule (with an optional disk-cache fast-path). Use this skill whenever the user wants to ungate, unlock, force-enable, or \"turn on\" a Claude Code feature hidden behind a flag — phrasings like \"ungate the X feature\", \"enable ultrareview\", \"unlock [feature name]\", \"turn on [feature]\", \"enable the workflow/agent tool\", \"this feature won't show up even with the env var\", \"force-enable a tengu_ flag\", \"flip the flag for X\", \"intercept the feature flag\", \"unlock a beta Claude Code feature\", or any case where a Claude Code feature is gated and the user wants it on. Also trigger when the user references a specific tengu_ flag name, or the existing \"GrowthBook Flag Enabler\" Proxyman rule, or wants to add another flag to it. For the user's own Claude Code install and account.\n- watch-next-jp\n- wisdom-to-acs-gap: Turn ONE video (YouTube URL/id or a pasted transcript) into an Agentic Coding School (ACS) content-gap report: extract its full wisdom, promote the 1-3 load-bearing \"spine\" ideas, deep-dive each in prose, gap-check each against the live ACS catalog, and pitch the buildable new ACS videos. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- claude-md-management:claude-md-improver\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- claude-in-chrome: Automates your Chrome browser to interact with web pages - clicking elements, filling forms, capturing screenshots, reading console logs, and navigating sites. (Minting happens only via grant-license, driven by\n    // the ACS claim flow — so nothing here can accidentally create a key.)\n    const all = await getAccountKeysByEmail(email);\n\n    // Display only active keys; revoked keys are hidden. Never log the full URL (the Gemini\n30\t    // endpoint embeds the API key in its query string) and never log the\n31\t    // upstream body (a provider could echo the request URL/key back in its\n32\t    // error payload). Clients receive only the status code, never the body.\n33\t    let target = \"upstream\";\n34\t    try {\n35\t      const { host, pathname } = new URL(url);\n36\t      target = `${host}${pathname}`;\n37\t    } catch {\n38\t      // keep generic label if url is unparseable\n39\t    }\n40\t    console.error(`[model-list] ${target} -> HTTP ${res.status}`);\n41\t    throw new Error(`HTTP ${res.status}`);\n42\t  }\n43\t  return res.json();\n44\t}\n45\t\n46\tfunction sortModels(models: Model[]): Model[] {\n47\t  return [...models].sort((a, b) => a.id.localeCompare(b.id));\n48\t}\n49\t\n50\tasync function fetchOpenAI(key: string): Promise<Model[]> {\n51\t  const data = (await apiGet(\"https://api.openai.com/v1/models\", {\n52\t    Authorization: `Bearer ${key}`,\n53\t  })) as { data?: Array<{ id: string }> };\n54\t  const models: Model[] = [];\n55\t  for (const m of data.data ?? []) {\n56\t    const id = m.id.toLowerCase();\n57\t    if (OPENAI_EXCLUDE.some((ex) => id.includes(ex))) continue;\n58\t    if (/-\\d{4}-\\d{2}-\\d{2}$/.test(m.id)) continue;\n59\t    if (m.id.endsWith(\"-chat-latest\")) continue;\n60\t    models.push({ id: m.id, display_name: m.id });\n61\t  }\n62\t  return sortModels(models);\n63\t}\n64\t\n65\tasync function fetchAnthropic(key: string): Promise<Model[]> {\n66\t  const all: Model[] = [];\n67\t  let url: string | null = \"https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models?limit=1000\";\n68\t  const headers = { \"x-api-key\": key, \"anthropic-version\": \"2023-06-01\" };\n69\t  while (url) {\n70\t    const data = (await apiGet(url, headers)) as {\n71\t      data?: Array<{ id: string; display_name?: string }>;\n72\t      has_more?: boolean;\n73\t    };\n74\t    for (const m of data.data ?? []) {\n75\t      all.push({ id: m.id, display_name: m.display_name ?? m.id });\n76\t    }\n77\t    if (data.has_more && data.data?.length) {\n78\t      const lastId = data.data[data.data.length - 1].id;\n79\t      url = `https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models?limit=1000&after_id=${encodeURIComponent(lastId)}`;\n80\t    } else {\n81\t      url = null;\n82\t    }\n83\t  }\n84\t  return sortModels(all);\n85\t}\n86\t\n87\tasync function fetchGemini(key: string): Promise<Model[]> {\n88\t  const all: Model[] = [];\n89\t  let url: string | null = `https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models?key=${encodeURIComponent(key)}&pageSize=1000`;\n90\t  while (url) {\n91\t    const data = (await apiGet(url)) as {\n92\t      models?: Array<{ name: string; displayName?: string; supportedGenerationMethods?: string[] }>;\n93\t      nextPageToken?: string;\n94\t    };\n95\t    for (const m of data.models ?? []) {\n96\t      const methods = m.supportedGenerationMethods ?? [];\n97\t      if (!methods.includes(\"generateContent\")) continue;\n98\t      const id = m.name.replace(/^models\\//, \"\");\n99\t      const lower = id.toLowerCase();\n100\t      if (GEMINI_EXCLUDE.some((kw) => lower.includes(kw))) continue;\n101\t      if (/preview-\\d{2}-\\d{4}$/.test(id)) continue;\n102\t      if (id.endsWith(\"-001\") || id.endsWith(\"-latest\")) continue;\n103\t      all.push({ id, display_name: m.displayName ?? id });\n104\t    }\n105\t    url = data.nextPageToken\n106\t      ? `https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models?key=${encodeURIComponent(key)}&pageSize=1000&pageToken=${encodeURIComponent(data.nextPageToken)}`\n107\t      : null;\n108\t  }\n109\t  return sortModels(all);\n110\t}\n111\t\n112\tasync function fetchGroq(key: string): Promise<Model[]> {\n113\t  const data = (await apiGet(\"https://api.groq.com/openai/v1/models\", {\n114\t    Authorization: `Bearer ${key}`,\n115\t  })) as { data?: Array<{ id: string }> };\n116\t  const models: Model[] = [];\n117\t  for (const m of data.data ?? []) {\n118\t    if (GROQ_EXCLUDE.some((ex) => m.id.toLowerCase().includes(ex))) continue;\n119\t    models.push({ id: m.id, display_name: m.id });\n120\t  }\n121\t  return sortModels(models);\n122\t}\n123\t\n124\tasync function fetchXAI(key: string): Promise<Model[]> {\n125\t  const data = (await apiGet(\"https://api.x.ai/v1/models\", {\n126\t    Authorization: `Bearer ${key}`,\n127\t  })) as { data?: Array<{ id: string }> };\n128\t  const models: Model[] = [];\n129\t  for (const m of data.data ?? []) {\n130\t    const id = m.id.toLowerCase();\n131\t    if (XAI_EXCLUDE.some((ex) => id.includes(ex))) continue;\n132\t    models.push({ id: m.id, display_name: m.id });\n133\t  }\n134\t  return sortModels(models);\n135\t}\n136\t\n137\tasync function fetchCerebras(key: string): Promise<Model[]> {\n138\t  const data = (await apiGet(\"https://api.cerebras.ai/v1/models\", {\n139\t    Authorization: `Bearer ${key}`,\n140\t  })) as { data?: Array<{ id: string }> };\n141\t  return sortModels((data.data ?? []).map((m) => ({ id: m.id, display_name: m.id })));\n142\t}\n143\t\n144\tconst PROVIDERS = [\n145\t  { name: \"openai\",    envKey: \"OPENAI_API_KEY\",    fetcher: fetchOpenAI },\n146\t  { name: \"anthropic\", envKey: \"ANTHROPIC_API_KEY\", fetcher: fetchAnthropic },\n147\t  { name: \"gemini\",    envKey: \"GEMINI_API_KEY\",    fetcher: fetchGemini },\n148\t  { name: \"groq\",      envKey: \"GROQ_API_KEY\",      fetcher: fetchGroq },\n149\t  { name: \"grok\",      envKey: \"XAI_API_KEY\",       fetcher: fetchXAI },\n150\t  { name: \"cerebras\",  envKey: \"CEREBRAS_API_KEY\",  fetcher: fetchCerebras },\n151\t] as const;\n152\t\n153\texport type AvailableModels = {\n154\t  fetchedAt: string;\n155\t  providers: Record<string, ProviderResult>;\n156\t};\n157\t\n158\tasync function fetchAvailableModelsUncached(): Promise<AvailableModels> {\n159\t  const entries = await Promise.all(\n160\t    PROVIDERS.map(async ({ name, envKey, fetcher }): Promise<[string, ProviderResult]> => {\n161\t      const key = process.env[envKey];\n162\t      if (!key) return [name, { ok: false, error: `missing ${envKey}` }];\n163\t      try {\n164\t        const models = await fetcher(key);\n165\t        return [name, { ok: true, models }];\n166\t      } catch (e) {\n167\t        return [name, { ok: false, error: e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e) }];\n168\t      }\n169\t    })\n170\t  );\n171\t\n172\t  return {\n173\t    fetchedAt: new Date().toISOString(),\n174\t    providers: Object.fromEntries(entries),\n175\t  };\n176\t}\n177\t\n178\t// Per-instance in-memory cache (~1h TTL). Bounds the 6-provider fan-out even\n179\t// against cache-busting query strings, which bypass Vercel's edge cache and\n180\t// hit the function directly. In-flight de-duplication collapses concurrent\n181\t// cold-start requests into a single upstream fan-out.\n182\tconst CACHE_TTL_MS = 60 * 60 * 1000;\n183\t// If every provider failed (e.g. a transient cold-start network blip), cache\n184\t// only briefly so a fully-broken response isn't pinned for the full hour.\n185\tconst FAILURE_CACHE_TTL_MS = 60 * 1000;\n186\tlet cache: { value: AvailableModels; expiresAt: number } | null = null;\n187\tlet inFlight: Promise<AvailableModels> | null = null;\n188\t\n189\texport async function fetchAvailableModels(): Promise<AvailableModels> {\n190\t  if (cache && cache.expiresAt > Date.now()) return cache.value;\n191\t  if (inFlight) return inFlight;\n192\t\n193\t  inFlight = (async () => {\n194\t    try {\n195\t      const value = await fetchAvailableModelsUncached();\n196\t      const anyOk = Object.values(value.providers).some((p) => p.ok);\n197\t      const ttl = anyOk ? CACHE_TTL_MS : FAILURE_CACHE_TTL_MS;\n198\t      cache = { value, expiresAt: Date.now() + ttl };\n199\t      return value;\n200\t    } finally {\n201\t      inFlight = null;\n202\t    }\n203\t  })();\n204\t\n205\t  return inFlight;\n206\t}\n207\t"}]},"uuid":"d7ee19ea-13ec-40ff-a0e8-612b236107a8","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:41:28.478Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/lib/services/model-list.ts","content":"const USER_AGENT = \"HyperWhisper-ModelSync/1.0\";\n\nconst OPENAI_EXCLUDE = [\n  \"whisper\", \"tts\", \"dall-e\", \"embedding\", \"moderation\", \"davinci\",\n  \"babbage\", \"realtime\", \"audio\", \"computer-use\", \"codex\", \"chatgpt\",\n  \"search\", \"transcribe\", \"gpt-image\", \"sora\", \"o1\", \"o3\", \"o4-mini\",\n  \"gpt-3.5\", \"gpt-4-\", \"gpt-4o\", \"gpt-4-turbo\", \"instruct\", \"-pro\",\n];\nconst GROQ_EXCLUDE = [\n  \"whisper\", \"tts\", \"distil\", \"tool-use\", \"guard\", \"safeguard\",\n  \"orpheus\", \"allam\", \"prompt-guard\",\n];\nconst GEMINI_EXCLUDE = [\n  \"imagen\", \"tts\", \"embedding\", \"aqa\", \"gemma\", \"learnlm\",\n  \"robotics\", \"computer-use\", \"deep-research\", \"image\",\n  \"nano-banana\", \"exp-\",\n];\nconst XAI_EXCLUDE = [\"image\", \"embedding\", \"tts\", \"audio\", \"vision\"];\n\nexport type Model = { id: string; display_name: string };\nexport type ProviderResult = { ok: true; models: Model[] } | { ok: false; error: string };\n\nasync function apiGet(url: string, headers: Record<string, string> = {}): Promise<unknown> {\n  const res = await fetch(url, {\n    headers: { \"User-Agent\": USER_AGENT, ...headers },\n    cache: \"no-store\",\n  });\n  if (!res.ok) {\n    // Log host + path + status only. Never log the full URL (the Gemini\n    // endpoint embeds the API key in its query string) and never log the\n    // upstream body (a provider could echo the request URL/key back in its\n    // error payload). Clients receive only the status code, never the body.\n    let target = \"upstream\";\n    try {\n      const { host, pathname } = new URL(url);\n      target = `${host}${pathname}`;\n    } catch {\n      // keep generic label if url is unparseable\n    }\n    console.error(`[model-list] ${target} -> HTTP ${res.status}`);\n    throw new Error(`HTTP ${res.status}`);\n  }\n  return res.json();\n}\n\nfunction sortModels(models: Model[]): Model[] {\n  return [...models].sort((a, b) => a.id.localeCompare(b.id));\n}\n\nasync function fetchOpenAI(key: string): Promise<Model[]> {\n  const data = (await apiGet(\"https://api.openai.com/v1/models\", {\n    Authorization: `Bearer ${key}`,\n  })) as { data?: Array<{ id: string }> };\n  const models: Model[] = [];\n  for (const m of data.data ?? []) {\n    const id = m.id.toLowerCase();\n    if (OPENAI_EXCLUDE.some((ex) => id.includes(ex))) continue;\n    if (/-\\d{4}-\\d{2}-\\d{2}$/.test(m.id)) continue;\n    if (m.id.endsWith(\"-chat-latest\")) continue;\n    models.push({ id: m.id, display_name: m.id });\n  }\n  return sortModels(models);\n}\n\nasync function fetchAnthropic(key: string): Promise<Model[]> {\n  const all: Model[] = [];\n  let url: string | null = \"https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models?limit=1000\";\n  const headers = { \"x-api-key\": key, \"anthropic-version\": \"2023-06-01\" };\n  while (url) {\n    const data = (await apiGet(url, headers)) as {\n      data?: Array<{ id: string; display_name?: string }>;\n      has_more?: boolean;\n    };\n    for (const m of data.data ?? []) {\n      all.push({ id: m.id, display_name: m.display_name ?? m.id });\n    }\n    if (data.has_more && data.data?.length) {\n      const lastId = data.data[data.data.length - 1].id;\n      url = `https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models?limit=1000&after_id=${encodeURIComponent(lastId)}`;\n    } else {\n      url = null;\n    }\n  }\n  return sortModels(all);\n}\n\nasync function fetchGemini(key: string): Promise<Model[]> {\n  const all: Model[] = [];\n  let url: string | null = `https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models?key=${encodeURIComponent(key)}&pageSize=1000`;\n  while (url) {\n    const data = (await apiGet(url)) as {\n      models?: Array<{ name: string; displayName?: string; supportedGenerationMethods?: string[] }>;\n      nextPageToken?: string;\n    };\n    for (const m of data.models ?? []) {\n      const methods = m.supportedGenerationMethods ?? [];\n      if (!methods.includes(\"generateContent\")) continue;\n      const id = m.name.replace(/^models\\//, \"\");\n      const lower = id.toLowerCase();\n      if (GEMINI_EXCLUDE.some((kw) => lower.includes(kw))) continue;\n      if (/preview-\\d{2}-\\d{4}$/.test(id)) continue;\n      if (id.endsWith(\"-001\") || id.endsWith(\"-latest\")) continue;\n      all.push({ id, display_name: m.displayName ?? id });\n    }\n    url = data.nextPageToken\n      ? `https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models?key=${encodeURIComponent(key)}&pageSize=1000&pageToken=${encodeURIComponent(data.nextPageToken)}`\n      : null;\n  }\n  return sortModels(all);\n}\n\nasync function fetchGroq(key: string): Promise<Model[]> {\n  const data = (await apiGet(\"https://api.groq.com/openai/v1/models\", {\n    Authorization: `Bearer ${key}`,\n  })) as { data?: Array<{ id: string }> };\n  const models: Model[] = [];\n  for (const m of data.data ?? []) {\n    if (GROQ_EXCLUDE.some((ex) => m.id.toLowerCase().includes(ex))) continue;\n    models.push({ id: m.id, display_name: m.id });\n  }\n  return sortModels(models);\n}\n\nasync function fetchXAI(key: string): Promise<Model[]> {\n  const data = (await apiGet(\"https://api.x.ai/v1/models\", {\n    Authorization: `Bearer ${key}`,\n  })) as { data?: Array<{ id: string }> };\n  const models: Model[] = [];\n  for (const m of data.data ?? []) {\n    const id = m.id.toLowerCase();\n    if (XAI_EXCLUDE.some((ex) => id.includes(ex))) continue;\n    models.push({ id: m.id, display_name: m.id });\n  }\n  return sortModels(models);\n}\n\nasync function fetchCerebras(key: string): Promise<Model[]> {\n  const data = (await apiGet(\"https://api.cerebras.ai/v1/models\", {\n    Authorization: `Bearer ${key}`,\n  })) as { data?: Array<{ id: string }> };\n  return sortModels((data.data ?? []).map((m) => ({ id: m.id, display_name: m.id })));\n}\n\nconst PROVIDERS = [\n  { name: \"openai\",    envKey: \"OPENAI_API_KEY\",    fetcher: fetchOpenAI },\n  { name: \"anthropic\", envKey: \"ANTHROPIC_API_KEY\", fetcher: fetchAnthropic },\n  { name: \"gemini\",    envKey: \"GEMINI_API_KEY\",    fetcher: fetchGemini },\n  { name: \"groq\",      envKey: \"GROQ_API_KEY\",      fetcher: fetchGroq },\n  { name: \"grok\",      envKey: \"XAI_API_KEY\",       fetcher: fetchXAI },\n  { name: \"cerebras\",  envKey: \"CEREBRAS_API_KEY\",  fetcher: fetchCerebras },\n] as const;\n\nexport type AvailableModels = {\n  fetchedAt: string;\n  providers: Record<string, ProviderResult>;\n};\n\nasync function fetchAvailableModelsUncached(): Promise<AvailableModels> {\n  const entries = await Promise.all(\n    PROVIDERS.map(async ({ name, envKey, fetcher }): Promise<[string, ProviderResult]> => {\n      const key = process.env[envKey];\n      if (!key) return [name, { ok: false, error: `missing ${envKey}` }];\n      try {\n        const models = await fetcher(key);\n        return [name, { ok: true, models }];\n      } catch (e) {\n        return [name, { ok: false, error: e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e) }];\n      }\n    })\n  );\n\n  return {\n    fetchedAt: new Date().toISOString(),\n    providers: Object.fromEntries(entries),\n  };\n}\n\n// Per-instance in-memory cache (~1h TTL). Bounds the 6-provider fan-out even\n// against cache-busting query strings, which bypass Vercel's edge cache and\n// hit the function directly. In-flight de-duplication collapses concurrent\n// cold-start requests into a single upstream fan-out.\nconst CACHE_TTL_MS = 60 * 60 * 1000;\n// If every provider failed (e.g. a transient cold-start network blip), cache\n// only briefly so a fully-broken response isn't pinned for the full hour.\nconst FAILURE_CACHE_TTL_MS = 60 * 1000;\nlet cache: { value: AvailableModels; expiresAt: number } | null = null;\nlet inFlight: Promise<AvailableModels> | null = null;\n\nexport async function fetchAvailableModels(): Promise<AvailableModels> {\n  if (cache && cache.expiresAt > Date.now()) return cache.value;\n  if (inFlight) return inFlight;\n\n  inFlight = (async () => {\n    try {\n      const value = await fetchAvailableModelsUncached();\n      const anyOk = Object.values(value.providers).some((p) => p.ok);\n      const ttl = anyOk ? CACHE_TTL_MS : FAILURE_CACHE_TTL_MS;\n      cache = { value, expiresAt: Date.now() + ttl };\n      return value;\n    } finally {\n      inFlight = null;\n    }\n  })();\n\n  return inFlight;\n}\n","numLines":207,"startLine":1,"totalLines":207}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"15956112-1f5a-4296-abd5-c791d1448371","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

fetch(`${baseUrl}/api/auth/get-session`, {\n      headers: {\n        cookie: request.headers.get(\"cookie\") || \"\",\n      },\n    });\n\n    if (!response.ok) return null;\n\n    const data = await response.json();\n    return data?.user ?? null;\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Middleware that handles:\n * 1. next-intl locale routing (adds locale prefix)\n * 2. Better Auth session checking\n * 3. User route protection (unified portal for customers and admins)\n */\nexport default async function middleware(request: NextRequest) {\n  const { pathname } = request.nextUrl;\n\n  // Check if this is a user route (matches /<locale>/user/*)\n  const isUserRoute = USER_ROUTE_REGEX.test(pathname);\n  const isUserSignIn = USER_SIGN_IN_REGEX.test(pathname);\n  const isUserAuthSignOut = USER_AUTH_SIGN_OUT_REGEX.test(pathname);\n  const isUserCustomers = USER_CUSTOMERS_REGEX.test(pathname);\n\n  // =============================================================\n  // USER ROUTES - Unified portal for customers and admins\n  // =============================================================\n\n  // For /user/customers, require admin access\n  if (isUserCustomers) {\n    const user = await getBetterAuthSession(request);\n\n    // If not authenticated, redirect to user sign-in\n    if (!user) {\n      const locale = getPathLocale(pathname);\n      const signInUrl = new URL(`/${locale}/user/sign-in`, request.url);\n      signInUrl.searchParams.set(\"returnTo\", pathname);\n      return NextResponse.redirect(signInUrl);\n    }\n\n    // Must be admin to access customers page\n    if (user.role !== \"admin\") {\n      const locale = getPathLocale(pathname);\n      return NextResponse.redirect(\n        new URL(`/${locale}/user/dashboard`, request.url)\n      );\n    }\n\n    // User is authenticated and is admin - run intl middleware\n    const response = intlMiddleware(request) as NextResponse;\n    response.headers.set(\"x-pathname\", pathname);\n    return response;\n  }\n\n  // For other user routes (except sign-in, sign-out), check authentication via cookie presence\n  if (\n    isUserRoute &&\n    !isUserSignIn &&\n    !isUserAuthSignOut\n  ) {\n    if (!hasSessionCookie(request)) {\n      const locale = getPathLocale(pathname);\n      const signInUrl = new URL(`/${locale}/user/sign-in`, request.url);\n      signInUrl.searchParams.set(\"returnTo\", pathname);\n      return NextResponse.redirect(signInUrl);\n    }\n\n    // User has session cookie - run intl middleware\n    const response = intlMiddleware(request) as NextResponse;\n    response.headers.set(\"x-pathname\", pathname);\n    return response;\n  }\n\n  // For user sign-in page, check if already authenticated\n  if (isUserSignIn) {\n    if (hasSessionCookie(request)) {\n      const locale = getPathLocale(pathname);\n      const returnTo = request.nextUrl.searchParams.get(\"returnTo\");\n      const redirectUrl = sanitizeReturnTo(returnTo, `/${locale}/user/dashboard`);\n      return NextResponse.redirect(new URL(redirectUrl, request.url));\n    }\n\n    const response = intlMiddleware(request) as NextResponse;\n    response.headers.set(\"x-pathname\", pathname);\n    return response;\n  }\n\n  // For all other routes, just run intl middleware\n  const response = intlMiddleware(request) as NextResponse;\n  response.headers.set(\"x-pathname\", pathname);\n\n  return response;\n}\n\nexport const config = {\n  // Match all pathnames except for:\n  // - API routes (/api/*)\n  // - Raw model inventory endpoint (/models)\n  // - Next.js internal files (/_next/*)\n  // - Static files with extensions (*.*)\n  // - Documentation (/docs/*)\n  matcher: [\"/((?!api|models(?:/|$)|_next|_vercel|docs|.*\\\\..*).*)\"],\n};\n","numLines":161,"startLine":1,"totalLines":161}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"dbac067e-ba4a-440b-adbf-6c660ffaf2c4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"3110ccea-69c4-4cb7-8200-a6aeb69b97d9","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77ec06fb-aafc-4d6b-b92c-3296ca279672","agentId":"a88ff7b2e1ac62eee","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019B8fH5eDSevXz1BpoGaGCq","type":"tool_result","content":"  const row = await db.query.accountKeys.findFirst({\n    where: eq(accountKeys.polarLicenseKeyId, polarLicenseKeyId),\n  });\n  return row ? drizzleAccountKeyToRow(row) : null;\n}\n\nexport async function getAccountKeysByEmail(email: string): Promise<AccountKeyRow[]> {\n  const rows = await db.query.accountKeys.findMany({\n    where: eq(accountKeys.email, email.toLowerCase()),\n    orderBy: [desc(accountKeys.createdAt)],\n  });\n  return rows.map(drizzleAccountKeyToRow);\n}\n\nexport async function getAllAccountKeysForAdmin(limit = 1000): Promise<AccountKeyRow[]> {\n  const rows = await db.query.accountKeys.findMany({\n    orderBy: [desc(accountKeys.createdAt)],\n    limit,\n  });\n  return rows.map(drizzleAccountKeyToRow);\n}\n\n/**\n * Distinct normalized emails that hold at least one *granted* Account Key (from\n * the internal bundle or a paid purchase). Powers the ACS admin backfill, which\n * reconciles its claim flag against emails that already have a key. Read-only.\n */\nexport async function getGrantedEmails(): Promise<string[]> {\n  const rows = await db\n    .selectDistinct({ email: accountKeys.email })\n    .from(accountKeys)\n    .where(eq(accountKeys.status, \"granted\"));\n  return rows\n    .map((r) => r.email.toLowerCase().trim())\n    .filter((email) => email.length > 0);\n}\n\nexport async function searchAccountKeysByEmail(\n  email: string,\n  limit = 1000\n): Promise<Array<AccountKeyRow & { credits: number }>> {\n  const rows = await db.query.accountKeys.findMany({\n    where: ilike(accountKeys.email, `%${email}%`),\n    orderBy: [desc(accountKeys.createdAt)],\n    limit,\n  });\n  const licenses = rows.map(drizzleAccountKeyToRow);\n  return attachPooledCredits(licenses);\n}\n\n// Credits granted with each internal-bundle Account Key mint. $10 at the\n// 1000-credits-per-dollar rate. This is the ACS member perk; only brand-new\n// emails receive it (an email that already has a granted key gets no top-up).\nconst INTERNAL_BUNDLE_CREDITS = 10000;\n\n/**\n * Mint a fresh internal-bundle license for an email: generate a collision-free\n * key, ensure the user exists, insert the license as \"granted\", and grant the\n * standard 10,000-credit ($10) internal bundle.\n *\n * This is the single source of truth for the internal mint flow. Its only\n * caller is the grant-license endpoint (driven by the ACS claim flow), which\n * mints only when the email has no granted key. licenses-for-email is read-only\n * and never mints. Throws on any failure.\n */\nexport async function provisionAccountKeyForEmail(email: string): Promise<AccountKeyRow> {\n  const normalizedEmail = email.toLowerCase().trim();\n\n  // Generate a unique license key with collision check\n  let key = \"\";\n  for (let i = 0; i < 5; i++) {\n    key = generateLicenseKey();\n    const collision = await findAccountByKey(key);\n    if (!collision) break;\n    if (i === 4) {\n      throw new Error(\"Failed to generate unique license key\");\n    }\n  }\n\n  const name = normalizedEmail.split(\"@\")[0];\n  const userResult = await getOrCreateUser(normalizedEmail, { name });\n  if (!userResult) {\n    throw new Error(\"Failed to create user\");\n  }\n\n  const license = await insertAccountKey({\n    key,\n    email: normalizedEmail,\n    userId: userResult.id,\n    status: \"granted\",\n  });\n  if (!license) {\n    throw new Error(\"Failed to insert license key\");\n  }\n\n  await grantCreditLot({\n    userId: license.userId,\n    amount: INTERNAL_BUNDLE_CREDITS,\n    sourceType: \"internal_bundle\",\n    sourceId: license.id,\n  });\n\n  return license;\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Credit Balances and Grants\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n// A handle that can run queries: either the pooled `db` connection or a\n// transaction handle. Used so balance reads can join the caller's transaction\n// instead of opening a second connection (which sees a stale snapshot and ties\n// up the pool while row locks are held).\ntype DbExecutor = typeof db | Parameters<Parameters<typeof db.transaction>[0]>[0];\n\n// A grant still backs spendable balance when it has no expiry or hasn't expired\n// yet. This is the single source of that rule for the raw-SQL credit queries\n// below (balance read, spend, clawback) — keep it here so expiry semantics live\n// in one place. The Drizzle-builder variant (using creditGrants.expiresAt) is\n// expressed inline where the query is built with the query builder.\nconst ACTIVE_GRANT_EXPIRY = sql`(expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > now())`;\n\n/**\n * Authoritative spendable balance: the SUM of remaining_amount across a\n * license's still-active grants. The credit_grants rows are the source of\n * truth; credit_balances is only a denormalized cache of this value. Reading\n * via the passed executor lets callers inside a FOR UPDATE transaction get a\n * consistent, locked view rather than a stale snapshot from the outer pool.\n */\nasync function getActiveGrantsTotal(\n  executor: DbExecutor,\n  userId: string\n): Promise<number> {\n  const result = await executor.execute<{ total: string | null }>(sql`\n    SELECT COALESCE(SUM(remaining_amount), 0) AS total\n    FROM credit_grants\n    WHERE user_id = ${userId}\n      AND status = 'active'\n      AND remaining_amount > 0\n      AND ${ACTIVE_GRANT_EXPIRY}\n  `);\n  return Number(result.rows[0]?.total ?? 0);\n}\n\n/**\n * Force the credit_balances cache to equal the authoritative grant total,\n * reconciling any drift between the two. Returns the reconciled balance. This\n * is the single place that heals divergence (e.g. a legacy balance row with no\n * matching grants, or a partially-applied transaction) so the cache can never\n * permanently show credits the grants can't back, or vice versa.\n */\nasync function reconcileCreditBalance(\n  tx: Parameters<Parameters<typeof db.transaction>[0]>[0],\n  userId: string\n): Promise<number> {\n  const total = await getActiveGrantsTotal(tx, userId);\n  await tx\n    .insert(creditBalances)\n    .values({ userId, balance: total.toString() })\n    .onConflictDoUpdate({\n      target: creditBalances.userId,","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"9322e96f-1cae-4dc5-b877-745186f8d002","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:41:43.279Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"  const row = await db.query.accountKeys.findFirst({\n    where: eq(accountKeys.polarLicenseKeyId, polarLicenseKeyId),\n  });\n  return row ? drizzleAccountKeyToRow(row) : null;\n}\n\nexport async function getAccountKeysByEmail(email: string): Promise<AccountKeyRow[]> {\n  const rows = await db.query.accountKeys.findMany({\n    where: eq(accountKeys.email, email.toLowerCase()),\n    orderBy: [desc(accountKeys.createdAt)],\n  });\n  return rows.map(drizzleAccountKeyToRow);\n}\n\nexport async function getAllAccountKeysForAdmin(limit = 1000): Promise<AccountKeyRow[]> {\n  const rows = await db.query.accountKeys.findMany({\n    orderBy: [desc(accountKeys.createdAt)],\n    limit,\n  });\n  return rows.map(drizzleAccountKeyToRow);\n}\n\n/**\n * Distinct normalized emails that hold at least one *granted* Account Key (from\n * the internal bundle or a paid purchase). (table) => [\n    uniqueIndex(\"idx_account_keys_stripe_session\").on(table.stripeSessionId),\n    // Backs the read-then-write dedupe in importLicenseFromPolar: a unique\n    // index on the stable (${attempts})`);\n\n    if (attempts >= maxAttempts) {\n      throw new Error(\"Failed to generate unique license key after max attempts\");\n    }\n  } while (true);\n\n  console.log(`Generated stripeCustomerId,\n  });\n\n  if (!user) {\n    throw new Error(`Failed to create user for ${customerEmail}`);\n  }\n\n  console.log(`User ready for ${customerEmail}: ${user.id}`);\n\n  // STEP 4: Store license in database\n  let insertedLicense;\n  try {\n    insertedLicense = await insertAccountKey({\n      key: licenseKey,\n      email: customerEmail.toLowerCase().trim(),\n      userId: user.id,\n      stripeCustomerId,\n      stripeSessionId: session.id,\n      status: \"granted\",\n    });\n  } catch (insertError: unknown) {\n    // Check if it's a duplicate (race condition with webhook retry)\n    if (\n      insertError &&\n      typeof insertError === \"object\" &&\n      \"code\" in insertError &&\n      (insertError as { code: string }).code === \"23505\"\n    ) {\n      console.log(\"License already inserted by concurrent request\");\n      return;\n    }\n    console.error(\"Failed to store license key:\", insertError);\n    throw insertError;\n  }\n\n  console.log(`License key stored in database for ${customerEmail}`);\n\n  // STEP 4b: Grant initial credits\n  if (insertedLicense) {\n    try {\n      await grantCreditLot({\n        userId: {"parentUuid":"65d7a517-e980-4ecd-aaa2-aebb5a77d78d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77ec06fb-aafc-4d6b-b92c-3296ca279672","agentId":"a88ff7b2e1ac62eee","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01QxXvPWDDUxDyJXHzVwTZgu","type":"tool_result","content":"import { polarClient, POLAR_ORGANIZATION_ID } from \"@/lib/clients/polar\";\nimport {\n  findAccountByKey,\n  findAccountById,\n  findAccountByPolarLicenseKeyId,\n  insertAccountKey,\n  grantCreditLot,\n  getOrCreateUser,\n  type AccountKeyRow,\n} from \"@/src/lib/db-layer\";\n\n/**\n * Shared license key validation logic.\n *\n * Used by both /api/license/validate and /api/license/activate so that\n * every entry point performs a real database check (with Polar fallback)\n * instead of trusting the key blindly.\n */\n\n/**\n * Import a license from Polar into the local database.\n *\n * Called when a license key is not found in the database but may exist in Polar.\n * Creates the license record, user (if needed), and grants 5000 credits.\n */\nasync function importLicenseFromPolar(licenseKey: string): Promise<{\n  success: boolean;\n  licenseId?: string;\n  error?: string;\n}> {\n  try {\n    // 1. Validate with Polar API\n    const polarResult = await polarClient.customerPortal.licenseKeys.validate({\n      key: licenseKey,\n      organizationId: POLAR_ORGANIZATION_ID,\n    });\n\n    // 2. Check if valid\n    if (polarResult.status !== \"granted\") {\n      return { success: false, error: `License is ${polarResult.status}` };\n    }\n\n    // 2b. Dedupe by the stable Polar license-key id (casing-independent).\n    // findAccountByKey is a case-sensitive exact match, so a different casing\n    // or whitespace variant of an already-imported key misses the DB lookup\n    // and reaches this fallback. Without this guard each variant would insert a\n    // fresh license row and grant another 5000 credits. polarResult.id is the\n    // canonical resource id regardless of how the input key was cased.\n    const alreadyImported = await findAccountByPolarLicenseKeyId(polarResult.id);\n    if (alreadyImported) {\n      return { success: true, licenseId: alreadyImported.id };\n    }\n\n    // 3. Get customer email - required for import\n    const email = polarResult.customer?.email;\n    if (!email) {\n      return { success: false, error: \"License has no associated email\" };\n    }\n\n    // 4. Get or create user\n    const user = await getOrCreateUser(email, {\n      name: polarResult.customer?.name ?? undefined,\n      polarCustomerId: polarResult.customerId ?? undefined,\n    });\n    if (!user) {\n      return { success: false, error: \"Failed to get or create user\" };\n    }\n\n    // 5. Insert license into database\n    const license = await insertAccountKey({\n      key: licenseKey,\n      email: email.toLowerCase().trim(),\n      userId: user.id,\n      polarLicenseKeyId: polarResult.id,\n      polarCustomerId: polarResult.customerId ?? null,\n      status: \"granted\",\n    });\n\n    if (!license) {\n      console.error(\"Failed to insert license from Polar\");\n      return { success: false, error: \"Failed to import license\" };\n    }\n\n    // 6. Create credit balance with 5000 credits\n    try {\n      await grantCreditLot({\n        userId: license.userId,\n        amount: 5000,\n        sourceType: \"polar_bundle\",\n        sourceId: polarResult.id,\n      });\n    } catch (creditError) {\n      console.error(\"Failed to create credit balance:\", creditError);\n      // License was created, but credits failed - still return success\n    }\n\n    console.log(\n      `Imported license from Polar: ${licenseKey} for ${email} with 5000 credits`\n    );\n\n    return { success: true, licenseId: license.id };\n  } catch (err) {\n    console.error(\"Polar license import error:\", err);\n    return {\n      success: false,\n      error: \"Failed to validate with Polar\",\n    };\n  }\n}\n\n===dblayer 100-140===\n    stripeSessionId: row.stripeSessionId,\n    createdAt: row.createdAt,\n  };\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// License Keys\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport async function insertAccountKey(data: AccountKeyInsert): Promise<AccountKeyRow | null> {\n  const polarLicenseKeyId = data.polarLicenseKeyId ?? null;\n  const insert = db\n    .insert(accountKeys)\n    .values({\n      key: data.key,\n      email: data.email,\n      userId: data.userId,\n      status: data.status ?? \"granted\",\n      polarLicenseKeyId,\n      polarCustomerId: data.polarCustomerId ?? null,\n      stripeCustomerId: data.stripeCustomerId ?? null,\n      stripeSessionId: data.stripeSessionId ?? null,\n    });\n\n  // When importing a Polar license, the unique index on polar_license_key_id\n  // (idx_license_keys_polar_license_key_id) is the authoritative dedupe guard:\n  // if a concurrent import already inserted a row for this Polar key, skip the\n  // insert and return the existing row instead of failing or creating a\n  // duplicate. The read-then-write check in importLicenseFromPolar handles the\n  // common case; this makes the rare race deterministic.\n  const [row] = polarLicenseKeyId\n    ? await insert\n        .onConflictDoNothing({ target: accountKeys.polarLicenseKeyId })\n        .returning()\n    : await insert.returning();\n\n  if (!row) {\n    return polarLicenseKeyId\n      ? await findAccountByPolarLicenseKeyId(polarLicenseKeyId)\n      : null;\n  }\n===getOrCreateUser===\n  });\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Users\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport async function getOrCreateUser(\n  email: string,\n  metadata?: { name?: string; stripeCustomerId?: string; polarCustomerId?: string }\n): Promise<UserResult | null> {\n  // Check if user exists\n  const existing = await db.query.user.findFirst({\n    where: eq(user.email, email.toLowerCase().trim()),\n  });\n  if (existing) return { id: existing.id, email: existing.email };\n\n  // Create via Better Auth's user table.\n  // emailVerified stays false: this address comes from a Stripe/Polar checkout\n  // or an admin/import grant, so the mailbox owner has never proven control of\n  // it. The magic-link plugin flips emailVerified to true on the first\n  // successful sign-in, which is the only point where ownership is verified.\n  const userId = crypto.randomUUID();\n  const [newUser] = await db\n    .insert(user)\n    .values({\n      id: userId,\n      name: metadata?.name ?? email.split(\"@\")[0],\n      email: email.toLowerCase().trim(),\n      emailVerified: false,\n    })\n    .returning();\n  if (!newUser) return null;\n\n  // Also insert an account record so the user can sign in via magic link\n  await db.insert(account).values({\n    id: `magic-link-${userId}`,\n    accountId: userId,\n    providerId: \"magic-link\",\n    userId: userId,\n    createdAt: new Date(),\n    updatedAt: new Date(),\n  });\n\n  return { id: newUser.id, email: newUser.email };\n}\n\nexport async function getUserById(id: string): Promise<UserResult | null> {\n  const existing = await db.query.user.findFirst({\n    where: eq(user.id, id),\n  });\n  return existing ? { id: existing.id, email: existing.email } : null;\n}\n\nexport async function getUserByEmail(email: string): Promise<UserResult | null> {\n  const existing = await db.query.user.findFirst({\n    where: eq(user.email, email.toLowerCase().trim()),\n  });\n  return existing ? { id: existing.id, email: existing.email } : null;\n}\n\nexport async function getUsersByIds(ids: string[]): Promise<Map<string, UserResult>> {\n  if (ids.length === 0) return new Map();\n  const rows = await db.query.user.findMany({\n    where: inArray(user.id, ids),\n  });\n  const map = new Map<string, UserResult>();\n  for (const row of rows) {\n    map.set(row.id, { id: row.id, email: row.email });\n  }\n  return map;\n}\n\n/**\n * Move a customer to a new email. The magic-link plugin re-verifies on the next sign-in.\n    await tx\n      .update(user)\n      .set({ email, emailVerified: false, updatedAt: new Date() })\n      .where(eq(user.id, userId));\n    await tx\n      .update(accountKeys)\n      .set({ email })\n      .where(eq(accountKeys.userId, userId));\n  });\n}","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"13b00ac5-c8bb-4151-b737-eafe6159770c","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:42:17.500Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"import { polarClient, POLAR_ORGANIZATION_ID } from \"@/lib/clients/polar\";\nimport {\n  findAccountByKey,\n  findAccountById,\n  findAccountByPolarLicenseKeyId,\n  insertAccountKey,\n  grantCreditLot,\n  getOrCreateUser,\n  type AccountKeyRow,\n} from \"@/src/lib/db-layer\";\n\n/**\n * Shared license key validation logic.\n *\n * Used by both /api/license/validate and /api/license/activate so that\n * every entry point performs a real database check (with Polar fallback)\n * instead of trusting the key blindly.\n */\n\n/**\n * Import a license from Polar into the local database.\n *\n * Called when a license key is not found in the database but may exist in Polar.\n * Creates the license record, user (if needed), and grants 5000 credits.\n */\nasync function importLicenseFromPolar(licenseKey: string): Promise<{\n  success: boolean;\n  licenseId?: string;\n  error?: string;\n}> {\n  try {\n    // 1. Validate with Polar API\n    const polarResult = await polarClient.customerPortal.licenseKeys.validate({\n      key: licenseKey,\n      organizationId: POLAR_ORGANIZATION_ID,\n    });\n\n    // 2. Check if valid\n    if (polarResult.status !== \"granted\") {\n      return { success: false, error: `License is ${polarResult.status}` };\n    }\n\n    // 2b. Dedupe by the stable Polar license-key id (casing-independent).\n    // findAccountByKey is a case-sensitive exact match, so a different casing\n    // or whitespace variant of an already-imported key misses the DB lookup\n    // and reaches this fallback. Without this guard each variant would insert a\n    // fresh license row and grant another 5000 credits. polarResult.id is the\n    // canonical resource id regardless of how the input key was cased.\n    const alreadyImported = await findAccountByPolarLicenseKeyId(polarResult.id);\n    if (alreadyImported) {\n      return { success: true, licenseId: alreadyImported.id };\n    }\n\n    // 3. Get customer email - required for import\n    const email = polarResult.customer?.email;\n    if (!email) {\n      return { success: false, error: \"License has no associated email\" };\n    }\n\n    // 4. Get or create user\n    const user = await getOrCreateUser(email, {\n      name: polarResult.customer?.name ?? undefined,\n      polarCustomerId: polarResult.customerId ?? undefined,\n    });\n    if (!user) {\n      return { success: false, error: \"Failed to get or create user\" };\n    }\n\n    // 5. Insert license into database\n    const license = await insertAccountKey({\n      key: licenseKey,\n      email: email.toLowerCase().trim(),\n      userId: user.id,\n      polarLicenseKeyId: polarResult.id,\n      polarCustomerId: polarResult.customerId ?? null,\n      status: \"granted\",\n    });\n\n    if (!license) {\n      console.error(\"Failed to insert license from Polar\");\n      return { success: false, error: \"Failed to import license\" };\n    }\n\n    // 6. Create credit balance with 5000 credits\n    try {\n      await grantCreditLot({\n        userId: license.userId,\n        amount: 5000,\n        sourceType: \"polar_bundle\",\n        sourceId: polarResult.id,\n      });\n    } catch (creditError) {\n      console.error(\"Failed to create credit balance:\", creditError);\n      // License was created, but credits failed - still return success\n    }\n\n    console.log(\n      `Imported license from Polar: ${licenseKey} for ${email} with 5000 credits`\n    );\n\n    return { success: true, licenseId: license.id };\n  } catch (err) {\n    console.error(\"Polar license import error:\", err);\n    return {\n      success: false,\n      error: \"Failed to validate with Polar\",\n    };\n  }\n}\n\n===dblayer 100-140===\n    stripeSessionId: row.stripeSessionId,\n    createdAt: row.createdAt,\n  };\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// License Keys\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport async function insertAccountKey(data: AccountKeyInsert): Promise<AccountKeyRow | null> {\n  const polarLicenseKeyId = data.polarLicenseKeyId ?? null;\n  const insert = db\n    .insert(accountKeys)\n    .values({\n      key: data.key,\n      email: data.email,\n      userId: data.userId,\n      status: data.status ?? \"granted\",\n      polarLicenseKeyId,\n      polarCustomerId: data.polarCustomerId ?? null,\n      stripeCustomerId: data.stripeCustomerId ?? null,\n      stripeSessionId: data.stripeSessionId ?? null,\n    });\n\n  // When importing a Polar license, the unique index on polar_license_key_id\n  // (idx_license_keys_polar_license_key_id) is the authoritative dedupe guard:\n  // if a concurrent import already inserted a row for this Polar key, skip the\n  // insert and return the existing row instead of failing or creating a\n  // duplicate. {"parentUuid":"b145bb6d-8872-4110-a0b8-577fc4366184","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77ec06fb-aafc-4d6b-b92c-3296ca279672","agentId":"a88ff7b2e1ac62eee","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_011ukZBLgm25yjZUNS11SpP8","type":"tool_result","content":"app/api/internal/granted-emails/route.ts:14:  if (!timingSafeEqualSecret(secret, process.env.HYPERWHISPER_INTERNAL_SECRET)) {\napp/api/internal/licenses-for-email/route.ts:11:  if (!timingSafeEqualSecret(secret, process.env.HYPERWHISPER_INTERNAL_SECRET)) {\napp/api/internal/grant-license/route.ts:11:  if (!timingSafeEqualSecret(secret, process.env.HYPERWHISPER_INTERNAL_SECRET)) {\r\n=== schema user email ===\n8:  email: text(\"email\").notNull().unique(),\n9:  emailVerified: boolean(\"email_verified\").notNull().default(false),\n=== sent-emails ===\naccount-keys.ts\nauth.ts\nblog-posts.ts\ncredit-balances.ts\ncredit-grants.ts\ndevice-validations.ts\nemails.ts\nindex.ts\nsent-emails.ts\nstripe-processed-events.ts\n=== license-key gen ===\n/**\n * License Key Generation Service\n *\n * Generates secure, unique license keys for HyperWhisper.\n * Format: HW-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX (19 characters total)\n *\n * DESIGN DECISIONS:\n * - Prefix \"HW-\" identifies HyperWhisper keys visually\n * - Base32 alphabet without ambiguous chars (0/O, 1/I/L) reduces user errors\n * - Uses crypto.randomInt for unbiased cryptographic randomness\n * - 4 segments of 4 chars each for readability\n */\nimport crypto from \"crypto\";\n\n// Base32-like alphabet without ambiguous characters\n// Excludes: 0 (zero), O (oh), 1 (one), I (eye), L (el)\nconst ALPHABET = \"ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTUVWXYZ23456789\"; // 31 chars\n\n/**\n * Generates a cryptographically secure license key.\n *\n * @returns A license key in format HW-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX\n *\n * ENTROPY CALCULATION:\n * - 16 characters from 31-char alphabet = 16 * log2(31) ≈ 79 bits\n * - Each character is drawn uniformly via crypto.randomInt (no modulo bias)\n * - Total effective entropy: ~79 bits (sufficient for license keys)\n */\nexport function generateLicenseKey(): string {\n  const MAX_RETRIES = 3;\n\n  for (let attempt = 0; attempt < MAX_RETRIES; attempt++) {\n    try {\n      let key = \"HW\";\n\n      // Generate 4 segments of 4 characters each\n      for (let segment = 0; segment < 4; segment++) {\n        key += \"-\";\n\n        for (let char = 0; char < 4; char++) {\n          // Draw a uniform index in [0, ALPHABET.length).\n          // crypto.randomInt performs rejection sampling internally, so the\n          // distribution is unbiased — unlike `byte % ALPHABET.length`, which\n          // over-represented the first (256 % 31) = 8 alphabet symbols.\n          const charIndex = crypto.randomInt(ALPHABET.length);\n\n          // Validate charIndex is within alphabet bounds\n          if (charIndex < 0 || charIndex >= ALPHABET.length) {\n            throw new Error(\n              `Character index ${charIndex} out of bounds (alphabet length: ${ALPHABET.length})`\n            );\n          }\n\n          const alphabetChar = ALPHABET[charIndex];\n\n          // Final safety check: ensure we got a valid character\n          if (!alphabetChar || typeof alphabetChar !== \"string\" || alphabetChar.length !== 1) {\n            throw new Error(\n              `Invalid character at index ${charIndex}: got ${alphabetChar} (type: ${typeof alphabetChar})`\n            );\n          }\n\n          key += alphabetChar;\n        }\n      }\n\n      // Validate the generated key format before returning\n      if (!isValidKeyFormat(key)) {\n        throw new Error(`Generated key failed format validation: ${key}`);\n      }\n\n      return key;\n    } catch (error) {\n      // If this is the last attempt, throw the error\n      if (attempt === MAX_RETRIES - 1) {\n        throw new Error(\n          `Failed to generate valid license key after ${MAX_RETRIES} attempts: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`\n        );\n      }\n      // Otherwise, log and retry\n      console.warn(\n        `License key generation attempt ${attempt + 1} failed, retrying...`,\n        error\n      );\n    }\n  }\n\n  // This should never be reached due to the throw above, but TypeScript needs it\n  throw new Error(\"License key generation failed unexpectedly\");\n}\n\n/**\n * Validates that a license key matches the expected format.\n * Does NOT check database validity - only format validation.\n *\n * @param key - The license key to validate\n * @returns true if format is valid, false otherwise\n */\nexport function isValidKeyFormat(key: string): boolean {\n  if (!key || typeof key !== \"string\") {\n    return false;\n  }\n\n  // Format: HW-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX\n  // Where X is any character from ALPHABET\n  const pattern = /^HW-[A-HJ-NP-Z2-9]{4}-[A-HJ-NP-Z2-9]{4}-[A-HJ-NP-Z2-9]{4}-[A-HJ-NP-Z2-9]{4}$/;\n  return pattern.test(key.toUpperCase());\n}","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"4566c645-8ec3-4ac2-9149-245b81453c02","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:43:01.139Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"app/api/internal/granted-emails/route.ts:14:  if (!timingSafeEqualSecret(secret, process.env.HYPERWHISPER_INTERNAL_SECRET)) {\napp/api/internal/licenses-for-email/route.ts:11:  if (!timingSafeEqualSecret(secret, process.env.HYPERWHISPER_INTERNAL_SECRET)) {\napp/api/internal/grant-license/route.ts:11:  if (!timingSafeEqualSecret(secret, process.env.HYPERWHISPER_INTERNAL_SECRET)) {\r\n=== schema user email ===\n8:  email: text(\"email\").notNull().unique(),\n9:  emailVerified: boolean(\"email_verified\").notNull().default(false),\n=== sent-emails ===\naccount-keys.ts\nauth.ts\nblog-posts.ts\ncredit-balances.ts\ncredit-grants.ts\ndevice-validations.ts\nemails.ts\nindex.ts\nsent-emails.ts\nstripe-processed-events.ts\n=== license-key gen ===\n/**\n * License Key Generation Service\n *\n * Generates secure, unique license keys for HyperWhisper.\n * Format: HW-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX (19 characters total)\n *\n * DESIGN DECISIONS:\n * - Prefix \"HW-\" identifies HyperWhisper keys visually\n * - Base32 alphabet without ambiguous chars (0/O, 1/I/L) reduces user errors\n * - Uses crypto.randomInt for unbiased cryptographic randomness\n * - 4 segments of 4 chars each for readability\n */\nimport crypto from \"crypto\";\n\n// Base32-like alphabet without ambiguous characters\n// Excludes: 0 (zero), O (oh), 1 (one), I (eye), L (el)\nconst ALPHABET = \"ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTUVWXYZ23456789\"; // 31 chars\n\n/**\n * Generates a cryptographically secure license key.\n *\n * @returns A license key in format HW-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX\n *\n * ENTROPY CALCULATION:\n * - 16 characters from 31-char alphabet = 16 * log2(31) ≈ 79 bits\n * - Each character is drawn uniformly via crypto.randomInt (no modulo bias)\n * - Total effective entropy: ~79 bits (sufficient for license keys)\n */\nexport function generateLicenseKey(): string {\n  const MAX_RETRIES = 3;\n\n  for (let attempt = 0; attempt < MAX_RETRIES; attempt++) {\n    try {\n      let key = \"HW\";\n\n      // Generate 4 segments of 4 characters each\n      for (let segment = 0; segment < 4; segment++) {\n        key += \"-\";\n\n        for (let char = 0; char < 4; char++) {\n          // Draw a uniform index in [0, ALPHABET.length).\n          // crypto.randomInt performs rejection sampling internally, so the\n          // distribution is unbiased — unlike `byte % ALPHABET.length`, which\n          // over-represented the first (256 % 31) = 8 alphabet symbols.\n          const charIndex = crypto.randomInt(ALPHABET.length);\n\n          // Validate charIndex is within alphabet bounds\n          if (charIndex < 0 || charIndex >= ALPHABET.length) {\n            throw new Error(\n              `Character index ${charIndex} out of bounds (alphabet length: ${ALPHABET.length})`\n            );\n          }\n\n          const alphabetChar = ALPHABET[charIndex];\n\n          // Final safety check: ensure we got a valid character\n          if (!alphabetChar || typeof alphabetChar !== \"string\" || alphabetChar.length !== 1) {\n            throw new Error(\n              `Invalid character at index ${charIndex}: got ${alphabetChar} (type: ${typeof alphabetChar})`\n            );\n          }\n\n          key += alphabetChar;\n        }\n      }\n\n      // Validate the generated key format before returning\n      if (!isValidKeyFormat(key)) {\n        throw new Error(`Generated key failed format validation: ${key}`);\n      }\n\n      return key;\n    } catch (error) {\n      // If this is the last attempt, throw the error\n      if (attempt === MAX_RETRIES - only format validation.\n *\n * @param key - The license key to validate\n * @returns true if format is valid, false otherwise\n */\nexport function isValidKeyFormat(key: string): boolean {\n  if (!key || typeof key !== \"string\") {\n    return false;\n  }\n\n  // Format: HW-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX\n  // Where X is any character from ALPHABET\n  const pattern = /^HW-[A-HJ-NP-Z2-9]{4}-[A-HJ-NP-Z2-9]{4}-[A-HJ-NP-Z2-9]{4}-[A-HJ-NP-Z2-9]{4}$/;\n  return pattern.test(key.toUpperCase());\n}","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b145bb6d-8872-4110-a0b8-577fc4366184","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}
