{"parentUuid":"ecea7139-1a8b-4487-80c4-d9733342ed8a","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a88ff7b2e1ac62eee","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdWHwmQ8L42gUBjRjoXPJ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"## T9 findings — `x-internal-secret` cluster (C4) + license-mint primitive + C8 model list\n\nFirst, two **inventory corrections** that changed the trace:\n\n- **EP-12 sends no email and never touches `sent_emails`.** `grant-license/route.ts` is 44 lines: gate → parse → `getAccountKeysByEmail` → `provisionAccountKeyForEmail`. No `emailService`, no `logSentEmail`. The \"email throws after mint\" partial-failure question relocates to the **admin tRPC `grant`** path, where it is real (B8).\n- **Both collision-retry loops are correct.** `db-layer.ts:249-256` and `customers.ts:203-208` both check `if (!collision) break;` *before* `if (i === 4) throw`. On the final iteration a collision falls through to the throw. No unchecked key can be persisted from either. The inventory's `break`/`i===4` ordering claim does not hold.\n- **`timingSafeEqualSecret` fails closed.** `!received || !expected` treats `undefined`, `null`, and `\"\"` as falsy, so an unset or empty `HYPERWHISPER_INTERNAL_SECRET` rejects *every* request including `x-internal-secret: \"\"`. Absent header → `headers.get()` returns `null` → false. Duplicate headers → undici joins to `\"a, b\"` (a string, never an array) → length mismatch → false. `timingSafeEqual` is only reached with equal-length buffers. The `:13` early return leaks secret *length* via timing, which is not exploitable here. **No bug. There is no request that passes this gate with the env var unset.**\n\n---\n\n### B1 — Admin `grant` is the one writer that does not normalize `account_keys.email`; every reader does\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/server/api/routers/admin/customers.ts:196` — `.input(z.object({ email: z.string().email() }))`. Zod's `.email()` accepts uppercase and does not transform.\n2. `customers.ts:198` — `const { email } = input;` (raw, never lowercased; contrast `updateEmail` at `:145`, which does `input.newEmail.toLowerCase().trim()`).\n3. `customers.ts:211` — `getOrCreateUser(email, …)`; that function normalizes *internally* (`db-layer.ts:935`, `:951`), so `user.email` lands **lowercase**.\n4. `customers.ts:217-222` — `insertAccountKey({ key: key!, email, … })` writes the **raw-cased** string into `account_keys.email`.\n5. `db-layer.ts:186-192` — `getAccountKeysByEmail` queries `eq(accountKeys.email, email.toLowerCase())`. No `citext`, no `lower()` index; `idx_account_keys_email` (`account-keys.ts:41`) is a plain case-sensitive btree.\n\n**seam** — hop 4 → hop 5. `insertAccountKey` (`db-layer.ts:109`) is a dumb pass-through that assumes its caller normalized; `getAccountKeysByEmail` assumes the column is already lowercase. Every *other* writer honours that: Stripe license purchase `lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts:99` (`customerEmail.toLowerCase().trim()`), Stripe credit purchase `stripe-webhook.ts:359`, Polar import `src/lib/license-validation.ts:71`, `provisionAccountKeyForEmail` `db-layer.ts:246`, `updateCustomerEmail` `db-layer.ts:1010`. Admin `grant` is the sole divergence.\n\n**trigger_sketch** — admin calls tRPC `admin.customers.grant({ email: \"Ray@Example.com\" })` (accepted verbatim by zod). Then:\n```\nPOST /api/internal/grant-license\nx-internal-secret: <secret>\n{\"email\":\"ray@example.com\"}\n```\n\n**consequence** — three distinct user-visible failures from one row:\n- EP-12: `getAccountKeysByEmail(\"ray@example.com\")` returns `[]`, so the guard at `grant-license/route.ts:32` misses and `provisionAccountKeyForEmail` mints a **second granted key** plus a **second 10,000-credit `internal_bundle` grant** for the same person. `grantCreditLot`'s `(sourceType, sourceId)` dedup cannot catch it (see B2).\n- EP-36 `licenses-for-email` returns `{\"licenses\":[]}` for a customer who demonstrably holds a key — support sees \"no license\" for a paid/granted account.\n- EP-37 `granted-emails` **disagrees with EP-36**: `getGrantedEmails` (`db-layer.ts:207-217`) lowercases *after* the query, so it *does* emit `ray@example.com`. The ACS backfill marks the member as already-claimed while the portal shows nothing. Two internal endpoints, same secret, contradictory answers about the same email.\n- Stripe credit top-up pooling (`stripe-webhook.ts:325-333`) also misses the row and mints a fresh key instead of topping up.\n\n**confidence** — high (the divergence is textual and the readers are unambiguous; only whether an admin has ever typed a capital letter is uncertain).\n\n---\n\n### B2 — `provisionAccountKeyForEmail` defeats `grantCreditLot`'s idempotency guard by passing a self-minted `sourceId`\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/app/api/internal/grant-license/route.ts:31-35` — read `getAccountKeysByEmail`, `.find(status === \"granted\")`, return if found.\n2. `route.ts:38` → `db-layer.ts:245 provisionAccountKeyForEmail` — **not** in a transaction, no advisory lock, and `account_keys` has **no unique index on `email`** (`account-keys.ts:31-46` — unique only on `stripe_session_id`, `polar_license_key_id`, `key`).\n3. `db-layer.ts:265-270` — `insertAccountKey` → new row, new `license.id` (`defaultRandom()` uuid).\n4. `db-layer.ts:272-277` — `grantCreditLot({ sourceType: \"internal_bundle\", sourceId: license.id })`.\n5. `db-layer.ts:380-395` — dedup is `onConflictDoNothing({ target: [creditGrants.sourceType, creditGrants.sourceId] })`.\n\n**seam** — hop 3 → hop 4/5. `grantCreditLotInTransaction` is built around the assumption that `sourceId` is a **stable external idempotency key** — and every other caller honours it: `stripe-webhook.ts:127` passes `session.id`, `license-validation.ts:113` passes `polarResult.id`, `grantCreditsForStripeEvent` passes `stripeObjectId`. `provisionAccountKeyForEmail` passes a UUID that *this very call* just generated, so it is unique by construction and the ON CONFLICT clause is dead code on this path. The check-then-mint at hop 1→2 is the only thing preventing a double grant, and it is a non-atomic read against an unconstrained column.\n\n**trigger_sketch** — for an email whose `user` row already exists (any prior Stripe buyer, magic-link signup, or admin-granted customer) but has no granted key, fire the ACS claim twice concurrently:\n```\nPOST /api/internal/grant-license   x-internal-secret: <secret>   {\"email\":\"a@b.com\"}\nPOST /api/internal/grant-license   x-internal-secret: <secret>   {\"email\":\"a@b.com\"}   # same instant\n```\n\n**consequence** — two granted keys for one account and **20,000 credits instead of 10,000** ($20 of a $10 perk). Both keys validate; the customer sees two keys in the portal. Repeatable per double-fire. Note the sub-case where the `user` row does *not* yet exist: the two `getOrCreateUser` calls race on `user.email UNIQUE`, one throws Postgres 23505, which `provisionAccountKeyForEmail` does not catch (`db-layer.ts:260-263` only handles a `null` return), so it surfaces as an opaque `500 {\"error\":\"Internal server error\"}` on a first-time claim — the error contract makes a retryable race look like a server fault.\n\n**confidence** — medium-high (mechanism certain; depends on the ACS claim flow double-firing, e.g. double-click or an upstream retry).\n\n---\n\n### B3 — EP-12/EP-36 validate emptiness *before* trimming, so `\" \"` mints a license for the email `\"\"`\n\n**trace**\n1. `grant-license/route.ts:19` — `if (!email || typeof email !== \"string\")` → `\" \"` is a truthy string, passes.\n2. `route.ts:22` — `email = email.toLowerCase().trim()` → `\"\"`. No re-check.\n3. `route.ts:31` — `getAccountKeysByEmail(\"\")` → `eq(email, \"\")` → `[]`.\n4. `db-layer.ts:246` — `normalizedEmail = \"\"`; `db-layer.ts:259` — `name = \"\".split(\"@\")[0]` → `\"\"`.\n5. `db-layer.ts:260` → `getOrCreateUser(\"\")` inserts a `user` row with `email: \"\"`, `name: \"\"` (`user.email` is `text().notNull().unique()` — `\"\"` satisfies both).\n6. `db-layer.ts:265` inserts `account_keys.email = \"\"`, then `db-layer.ts:272` grants 10,000 credits.\n\n**seam** — hop 1 → hop 2. The validator runs on the pre-normalized value; the normalizer produces a value the validator would have rejected. Classic validate-then-normalize inversion. There is **no format check anywhere downstream** — `account_keys.email` and `user.email` are unconstrained `text` (`account-keys.ts:21`, `auth.ts:8`), and `provisionAccountKeyForEmail` accepts whatever it is handed.\n\n**trigger_sketch**\n```\nPOST /api/internal/grant-license\nx-internal-secret: <secret>\n{\"email\":\"   \"}          # also: \"\\t\", \"\\n\", \"@\", \"a\", \"not-an-email\"\n```\n\n**consequence** — a real, validating license key with 10,000 credits bound to a ghost account with an empty email, occupying the globally-unique `user.email = \"\"` slot. It is also *sticky*: any later `{\"email\":\"\\n\"}` hits hop 3, finds the granted row, and hands the **same key** back to a different caller. `getGrantedEmails` (`db-layer.ts:216`) filters `email.length > 0`, so the ACS backfill never sees it and it is invisible to reconciliation. `\"a\"` / `\"not-an-email\"` produce the same outcome with a non-empty junk address. Related boundary: a ~3000-char `email` reaches the `user.email` unique btree and blows the 2704-byte index-row limit, surfacing as a `500` rather than a `400`.\n\n**confidence** — medium-high for the mechanism; the exploit requires the internal secret, so the realistic path is an ACS-side bug forwarding a blank form field, not an attacker.\n\n---\n\n### B4 — `getGrantedEmails` applies `SELECT DISTINCT` before normalizing, so EP-37 can return the same email twice\n\n**trace**\n1. `granted-emails/route.ts:19` → `db-layer.ts:207 getGrantedEmails`.\n2. `db-layer.ts:211-214` — `selectDistinct({ email: accountKeys.email }).where(eq(status,\"granted\"))` — DISTINCT on the **raw** column.\n3. `db-layer.ts:215-217` — `.map(r => r.email.toLowerCase().trim()).filter(len > 0)` — normalization happens **after** the DISTINCT.\n\n**seam** — hop 2 → hop 3. The SQL layer promises distinctness over stored bytes; the JS layer collapses casing afterwards and the route's own docstring (`granted-emails/route.ts:6-7`) then promises the caller \"every **distinct** normalized email\". The two definitions of \"distinct\" differ by exactly the rows B1 creates.\n\n**trigger_sketch** — after any admin `grant({email:\"Ray@Example.com\"})` followed by an EP-12 mint for `ray@example.com`, call `POST /api/internal/granted-emails` with a valid secret.\n\n**consequence** — `{\"emails\":[\"ray@example.com\", …, \"ray@example.com\"]}`. Harmless if the ACS backfill upserts; if it counts, increments a claim counter, or writes an audit row per element, the member is double-processed. No `LIMIT` on this query either, so the response grows unbounded with the table.\n\n**confidence** — medium (duplicate emission is certain once B1 fires; blast radius depends on the external ACS consumer, which is out of repo).\n\n---\n\n### B5 — `model-list.ts` guarantees \"clients receive only the status code\" on one branch only; a malformed provider key escapes into the public JSON\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/lib/services/model-list.ts:28-42` — the `!res.ok` branch is carefully sanitized: it logs `${host}${pathname}` and throws `new Error(\"HTTP ${res.status}\")`, with a comment at `:29-32` stating *\"Clients receive only the status code, never the body.\"*\n2. That guarantee covers only errors raised **inside** the `!res.ok` block. `fetch()` itself is at `:24-27`, **before** it.\n3. `model-list.ts:163-168` —\n   ```ts\n   try { const models = await fetcher(key); return [name, { ok: true, models }]; }\n   catch (e) { return [name, { ok: false, error: e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e) }]; }\n   ```\n   This layer assumes *every* throw from `fetcher` is already sanitized.\n4. `model-list.ts:172-175` → `providers` object → `NextResponse.json(...)` at `app/models/route.ts:9` and `app/api/internal/models/route.ts:9`, both **anonymous**.\n\n**seam** — hop 1/2 → hop 3. `apiGet` sanitizes the HTTP-error path; `fetchAvailableModelsUncached` treats that as a whole-function guarantee. The five header-based providers pass the key straight into the request init: `Authorization: \\`Bearer ${key}\\`` (`:52`, `:114`, `:126`, `:139`) and `\"x-api-key\": key` (`:68`). Undici validates header *values* during request construction, before any network I/O, and puts the offending value verbatim in the message. Verified on node v22.14.0:\n```\nfetch(url, { headers: { \"x-api-key\": \"sk-ant-SECRETVALUE123\\rx\" } })\n  → TypeError: Headers.append: \"sk-ant-SECRETVALUE123x\" is an invalid header value.\n```\nThat message reaches `e.message` at `:167` and is serialized to the public body as `{\"anthropic\":{\"ok\":false,\"error\":\"Headers.append: \\\"sk-ant-…\\\" is an invalid header value.\"}}`.\n\n**trigger_sketch** — any deployment where `OPENAI_API_KEY` / `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` / `GROQ_API_KEY` / `XAI_API_KEY` / `CEREBRAS_API_KEY` contains an interior control character (CR mid-value from a CRLF-mangled `.env`, a stray char from a copy-paste). Then, unauthenticated: `GET /api/internal/models?cb=1`.\n\n**Negative results I chased and can rule out**, so this is scoped tightly:\n- Gemini is **safe** — its key is in the query string via `encodeURIComponent` (`:89`, `:106`), so a bad character is percent-encoded and returns `HTTP 400` through the sanitized path. The URL never appears in a message: all URLs are literals, so `TypeError: Failed to parse URL from …` (which *does* embed the full URL) is unreachable here.\n- Plain trailing/leading whitespace or a bare trailing `\\n` is **not** enough — undici trims it and the request goes out normally.\n- `res.json()` on a non-JSON 200 throws `Unexpected token '<', \"<html><hea\"... is not valid JSON` — node truncates the echoed body to **10 characters**, so no URL or key can escape that way. This still technically breaks the `:32` \"never the body\" promise, but not usefully.\n- DNS/connect failures give a bare `fetch failed`.\n\n**consequence** — an API key printed in a public, unauthenticated HTTP response. Requires a specific env misconfiguration, but the whole point of the `:29-32` comment is that this class of leak was supposed to be closed, and it is closed on only one of the two throw paths.\n\n**confidence** — low on the trigger occurring, high on the mechanism. The cheap fix is at the seam (`:167`), not at `apiGet`.\n\n---\n\n### B6 — EP-50 has no gate, no distinguishing behaviour from EP-49, and the in-memory cache is per-instance\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/app/api/internal/models/route.ts` is **byte-identical** to `nextjs/app/models/route.ts` — same `dynamic = \"force-dynamic\"`, same `Cache-Control: public, s-maxage=3600`, and crucially **no `x-internal-secret` check**, unlike its three siblings in `app/api/internal/` (`grant-license:11`, `licenses-for-email:11`, `granted-emails:14`).\n2. `nextjs/middleware.ts:159` — matcher `\"/((?!api|models(?:/|$)|_next|_vercel|docs|.*\\\\..*).*)\"` excludes both `/api/*` and `/models`. Confirmed: nothing else gates it. No `vercel.json` rewrite or header rule covers it either.\n3. `model-list.ts:186-187` — `let cache` / `let inFlight` are **module-level**, i.e. per lambda instance.\n\n**seam** — hop 1 → hop 3. The directory convention `app/api/internal/` is load-bearing everywhere else in this cluster and carries zero enforcement here; a reviewer scanning for \"which internal routes are gated\" sees three of four. Separately, the comment at `:11-14` of both route files reasons about the CDN absorbing anonymous traffic, but `dynamic = \"force-dynamic\"` plus a unique query string bypasses the edge cache and invokes the function; a cold instance has an empty module cache and does a full six-provider fan-out. The in-flight promise (`:191`, `:193-205`) de-dupes only *within one instance* — it cannot collapse requests spread across the instances Vercel spins up under concurrency.\n\n**trigger_sketch** — `for i in $(seq 1 500); do curl \"https://…/api/internal/models?cb=$i\" & done` from distributed sources.\n\n**consequence** — an anonymous caller drives up to 6 authenticated third-party API calls per cold instance (OpenAI, Anthropic incl. its pagination loop at `:69-83`, Gemini incl. its page loop, Groq, xAI, Cerebras), burning quota and potentially rate-limiting the keys for legitimate use. Bounded by instance scale-out, not by the cache. Also note `FAILURE_CACHE_TTL_MS = 60_000` (`:185`): during a real provider outage the TTL drops to 60s, so sustained traffic re-fans-out every minute exactly when upstreams are unhealthy.\n\n**confidence** — high that EP-50 is ungated and duplicates EP-49 verbatim (so it has no reason to exist); medium on the amplification magnitude.\n\n---\n\n### B8 — Admin `grant` is non-idempotent and swallows email failure\n\n**trace**\n1. `customers.ts:195-234` — no lookup for an existing key by email anywhere in the mutation (contrast `grant-license/route.ts:31-35`, which explicitly guards).\n2. `customers.ts:217` insert → `:228` `grantCreditLot({amount: 5000, sourceId: license.id})` → `:238` `await emailService.sendLicenseKey(...)`.\n3. `lib/services/email.ts:162-240` — `sendWithRetry` **never throws**; it returns `{ success: false, error }` after logging a `status:\"failed\"` row via `safeLogSentEmail` (`:227-233`).\n4. `customers.ts:246` returns `{ email, licenseKey }` unconditionally.\n\n**seam** — hop 2 → hop 3. The mutation `await`s `sendLicenseKey` as if a rejection would abort it, but the email layer converts every failure into a resolved `{success:false}`. `sent_emails` is an **append-only audit log** (`src/db/schema/sent-emails.ts:5-8`, indexes on `recipient`/`created_at` only, no unique constraint) — it is *not* a dedup key and nothing reads it before sending.\n\n**trigger_sketch** — admin grants to an address Resend rejects (suppressed recipient / invalid domain → `isRetryableResendError` returns false at `email.ts:213`, so it breaks after one attempt). The mutation returns success. Admin, seeing the customer never received it, clicks Grant again.\n\n**consequence** — each retry mints another key and another 5,000 credits (again, `sourceId: license.id` is fresh per call, so `grantCreditLot`'s dedup is inert — same root cause as B2). The admin UI shows success on a delivery that silently failed; the only signal is a `status:\"failed\"` row nobody queries.\n\n**confidence** — medium.\n\n---\n\n### B9 — Middleware matcher exclusions lack word boundaries (low)\n\n`middleware.ts:159`: `\"/((?!api|models(?:/|$)|_next|_vercel|docs|.*\\\\..*).*)\"`. `models` is boundary-anchored with `(?:/|$)`; `api`, `docs`, `_next`, `_vercel` are bare prefixes. So `/apidocs`, `/docs-v2`, `/apitest` skip the intl middleware entirely and never get locale-prefixed. **consequence**: any future top-level page whose slug starts with one of those four strings 404s instead of redirecting to `/en/…`. No current route hits it. **confidence**: high on the regex behaviour, low on impact.\n\n---\n\n### depth_limits\n\n- **Not traced:** the external ACS consumer of EP-12/36/37. Whether the claim flow can double-fire (B2), forwards blank emails (B3), or dedupes the EP-37 array (B4) is out of repo — I could only establish that the server side offers no protection.\n- **Not read in full:** `lib/templates/license-email.ts` (only reachable from the admin/Stripe paths, not EP-12), the `spendCreditGrants*` family, `device-validations`, `/api/license/validate` internals.\n- **No production/network verification.** The undici and `res.json()` error-message shapes in B5 were confirmed against a local node v22.14.0 and a throwaway localhost server with a fake string — no provider was contacted and no `.env` was read. Vercel's node version may differ, though these messages have been stable since node 18.\n- **Not verified:** actual DB contents.
